# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

```plantuml
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
```

# Operation Specifications
Session::replaceTrainer
Summary: Replaces the trainer currently assigned to this session with a
         new trainer, strictly before the session's start date.
Algorithm:
  1. If newTrainer is null or today is null, return false.
  2. If date is null, return false.
  3. If today is not strictly before date (i.e., today >= date), return
     false.
  4. Set trainer to newTrainer.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: Session.trainer
Related Classes: Trainer, Person
Input: newTrainer : Trainer - the new trainer to assign; must be
       non-null.
       today : LocalDate - the current date; must be strictly earlier
       than this session's date.
Output: boolean - true if the trainer was successfully replaced; false
        otherwise (invalid arguments, no session date, or today not
        strictly before date).
Pre-condition: The Session instance exists; today is a valid LocalDate
               strictly earlier than date; a trainer (any reference) is
               currently assigned.
Post-condition: On success, trainer is set to newTrainer; on failure,
                trainer is unchanged.

Session::assignTrainer
Summary: Assigns a trainer to this session, which must be done strictly
         before the session's start date.
Algorithm:
  1. If t is null or today is null, return false.
  2. If date is null, return false.
  3. If today is not strictly before date (i.e., today >= date), return
     false (same-day or past assignments are not allowed).
  4. If trainer is already set, return false (use replaceTrainer to change
     an existing trainer).
  5. Set trainer to t.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: Session.trainer
Related Classes: Trainer, Person
Input: t : Trainer - the trainer to assign; must be non-null.
       today : LocalDate - the current date; must be strictly earlier
       than this session's date.
Output: boolean - true if the trainer was successfully assigned; false
        otherwise (invalid arguments, no session date, today not
        strictly before date, or a trainer already assigned).
Pre-condition: The Session instance exists; today is a valid LocalDate
               strictly earlier than date; trainer is currently null.
Post-condition: On success, trainer is set to t; on failure, trainer is
                unchanged.

Session::registerParticipant
Summary: Registers a participant for this session, ensuring no duplicate
         enrollments for the same participant.
Algorithm:
  1. If p is null, return false.
  2. If registeredParticipants already contains p (matched by participant
     id), return false (no duplicate enrollments).
  3. Add p to registeredParticipants.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: Session.registeredParticipants
Related Classes: Participant, Person
Input: p : Participant - the participant to enroll; must be non-null and
       not already registered for this session.
       today : LocalDate - the current date (used for consistency with
       the registration workflow; this implementation does not impose
       a date-based restriction for participants).
Output: boolean - true if the participant was successfully registered;
        false otherwise (null participant or duplicate enrollment).
Pre-condition: The Session instance exists; p is a valid Participant not
               yet registered for this session.
Post-condition: On success, registeredParticipants contains p; on
                failure, registeredParticipants is unchanged.

```plantuml
class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
School::assignCourseToSector
Summary: Assigns a course to a sector, removing it from its current sector
         (if any) before adding it to the new one.
Algorithm:
  1. If courseId or sectorId is null/empty, return false.
  2. Find the course c in courses whose id equals courseId; if not found,
     return false.
  3. Find the sector s in sectors whose id equals sectorId; if not found,
     return false.
  4. If c.sector is not null and different from s, call
     c.sector.removeCourse(c) to detach c from its previous sector.
  5. Call s.addCourse(c) to attach c to the new sector.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: School.courses, School.sectors, Course.sector,
                  Sector.courses
Related Classes: Course, Sector
Input: courseId : String - the unique id of the course to move; must
       reference an existing course in this school.
       sectorId : String - the unique id of the target sector; must
       reference an existing sector in this school.
Output: boolean - true if the course was successfully (re)assigned to the
        target sector; false if any id is invalid or a referenced
        course/sector does not exist.
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; both courseId and sectorId
               refer to existing course and sector within this school.
Post-condition: On success, the course's sector is the target sector and
                the course appears in that sector's courses list (and is
                no longer present in any previous sector's courses list);
                on failure, course/sector memberships are unchanged.

School::addCourse
Summary: Adds a new course to the school by providing a case-sensitive
         unique course id.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (after trimming), return false.
  2. Iterate over courses; if any course has an id equal (case-sensitive)
     to the provided id, return false.
  3. Create a new Course with id, empty documents, null sector, and empty
     sessions.
  4. Add the new course to courses.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: School.courses
Related Classes: Course
Input: id : String - the unique case-sensitive identifier of the new
       course; must be non-null and not already used by an existing course.
Output: boolean - true if the course was successfully added; false
        otherwise.
Pre-condition: The School instance exists with a unique name; id is a
               non-null, non-empty String not currently used by any
               existing course in this school.
Post-condition: On success, courses contains a new Course with the given
                id; on failure, courses is unchanged.

School::addSector
Summary: Creates a new sector with a case-sensitive unique id and adds it
         to the school's list of sectors, returning the created sector.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (after trimming), return null.
  2. Iterate over sectors; if any sector has an id equal (case-sensitive)
     to the provided id, return null.
  3. Create a new Sector with id and an empty courses list.
  4. Add the new sector to sectors.
  5. Return the created sector.
Related Features: School.sectors
Related Classes: Sector
Input: id : String - the unique case-sensitive identifier of the new
       sector; must be non-null and not already used by an existing
       sector.
Output: Sector - the newly created Sector on success; null if the id is
        invalid or already in use.
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; id is a non-null, non-empty
               String not currently used by any existing sector in this
               school.
Post-condition: On success, sectors contains the new Sector and the
                returned reference refers to it; on failure, sectors is
                unchanged and null is returned.

3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Course::removeDocument
Summary: Deletes a referenced document from the course by removing it from
         the course's document list.
Algorithm:
  1. If doc is null, return false.
  2. If documents does not contain doc (matched by reference/name), return
     false.
  3. Remove doc from documents.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: Course.documents
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc : Document - the document to delete; must be non-null and
       currently present in this course's documents.
Output: boolean - true if the document was successfully removed; false
        otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists and currently references the
               given document.
Post-condition: On success, documents no longer contains the deleted
                document; on failure, documents is unchanged.

Course::addDocument
Summary: Uploads a referenced document to the course by appending it to the
         course's document list.
Algorithm:
  1. If doc is null, return false.
  2. If documents already contains an element with the same name as doc,
     return false (avoid duplicate document names on the same course).
  3. Add doc to documents.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: Course.documents
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc : Document - the document to upload; must be non-null and its
       name must not collide with an existing document name on this course.
Output: boolean - true if the document was successfully added; false
        otherwise (null document or duplicate document name).
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists in the school's catalog.
Post-condition: On success, documents contains the new Document; on
                failure, documents is unchanged.

Course::addSession
Summary: Adds a new session for this course on the specified date, ensuring
         that only one session exists per course per day, and returns the
         up-to-date list of sessions.
Algorithm:
  1. If date is null, return the current sessions unchanged.
  2. Iterate over sessions; if any session has the same date, return the
     current sessions unchanged (no duplicate date allowed).
  3. Create a new Session with date, empty registeredParticipants, and
     null trainer.
  4. Append the new session to sessions.
  5. Return the up-to-date sessions list.
Related Features: Course.sessions
Related Classes: Session
Input: date : LocalDate - the date of the new session in "yyyy-MM-dd" format.
Output: List<Session> - the course's updated list of sessions, containing
        the new session when the addition was successful, or the original
        list when the date was already taken or invalid.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; date is a valid LocalDate and
               no existing session of this course has the same date.
Post-condition: On success, sessions contains one additional Session with
                the provided date; on failure, sessions is unchanged.

Course::cancelSession
Summary: Cancels (removes) the session of this course that occurs on the
         given date, but only when it has no registered participants.
Algorithm:
  1. If date is null, return false.
  2. Find the session s in sessions whose date equals the given date.
  3. If no such session is found, return false.
  4. If s.registeredParticipants is not empty, return false
     (sessions with participants cannot be removed).
  5. Remove s from sessions.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: Course.sessions
Related Classes: Session, Participant
Input: date : LocalDate - the date of the session to cancel, format
       "yyyy-MM-dd".
Output: boolean - true if the session was successfully removed; false if
        the date is null, the session does not exist, or the session has
        one or more registered participants.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; date refers to an existing
               session of this course.
Post-condition: On success, the matching session is removed from
                sessions; on failure, sessions is unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Course::removeDocument
Summary: Deletes a referenced document from the course by removing it from
         the course's document list.
Algorithm:
  1. If doc is null, return false.
  2. If documents does not contain doc (matched by reference/name), return
     false.
  3. Remove doc from documents.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: Course.documents
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc : Document - the document to delete; must be non-null and
       currently present in this course's documents.
Output: boolean - true if the document was successfully removed; false
        otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists and currently references the
               given document.
Post-condition: On success, documents no longer contains the deleted
                document; on failure, documents is unchanged.

Course::addDocument
Summary: Uploads a referenced document to the course by appending it to the
         course's document list.
Algorithm:
  1. If doc is null, return false.
  2. If documents already contains an element with the same name as doc,
     return false (avoid duplicate document names on the same course).
  3. Add doc to documents.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: Course.documents
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc : Document - the document to upload; must be non-null and its
       name must not collide with an existing document name on this course.
Output: boolean - true if the document was successfully added; false
        otherwise (null document or duplicate document name).
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists in the school's catalog.
Post-condition: On success, documents contains the new Document; on
                failure, documents is unchanged.

Course::addSession
Summary: Adds a new session for this course on the specified date, ensuring
         that only one session exists per course per day, and returns the
         up-to-date list of sessions.
Algorithm:
  1. If date is null, return the current sessions unchanged.
  2. Iterate over sessions; if any session has the same date, return the
     current sessions unchanged (no duplicate date allowed).
  3. Create a new Session with date, empty registeredParticipants, and
     null trainer.
  4. Append the new session to sessions.
  5. Return the up-to-date sessions list.
Related Features: Course.sessions
Related Classes: Session
Input: date : LocalDate - the date of the new session in "yyyy-MM-dd" format.
Output: List<Session> - the course's updated list of sessions, containing
        the new session when the addition was successful, or the original
        list when the date was already taken or invalid.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; date is a valid LocalDate and
               no existing session of this course has the same date.
Post-condition: On success, sessions contains one additional Session with
                the provided date; on failure, sessions is unchanged.

Course::cancelSession
Summary: Cancels (removes) the session of this course that occurs on the
         given date, but only when it has no registered participants.
Algorithm:
  1. If date is null, return false.
  2. Find the session s in sessions whose date equals the given date.
  3. If no such session is found, return false.
  4. If s.registeredParticipants is not empty, return false
     (sessions with participants cannot be removed).
  5. Remove s from sessions.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: Course.sessions
Related Classes: Session, Participant
Input: date : LocalDate - the date of the session to cancel, format
       "yyyy-MM-dd".
Output: boolean - true if the session was successfully removed; false if
        the date is null, the session does not exist, or the session has
        one or more registered participants.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; date refers to an existing
               session of this course.
Post-condition: On success, the matching session is removed from
                sessions; on failure, sessions is unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Sector::addCourse
Summary: Adds a course to this sector's list of courses.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null, return without doing anything.
  2. If courses already contains course, return without doing anything
     (avoid duplicates).
  3. Add course to courses.
 Related Features: Sector.courses
Related Classes: Course
Input: course : Course - the course to attach to this sector; must be
       non-null and not already a member of this sector.
Output: void.
Pre-condition: The Sector instance exists; course is non-null and is not
               already in this sector's courses list.
Post-condition: On success, courses contains the given course; on
                failure, courses is unchanged.

Sector::removeCourse
Summary: Removes a course from this sector's list of courses and clears
         the course's sector reference when applicable.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null, return without doing anything.
  2. If courses does not contain course, return without doing anything.
  3. Remove course from courses.
  4. If course.sector equals this sector, set course.sector to null to
     keep both sides consistent.
Related Features: Sector.courses, Course.sector
Related Classes: Course
Input: course : Course - the course to detach from this sector; must be
       non-null and currently a member of this sector.
Output: void.
Pre-condition: The Sector instance exists; course is currently in this
               sector's courses list.
Post-condition: On success, courses no longer contains the given course
                and the course's sector reference is cleared (if it was
                pointing to this sector); on failure, memberships are
                unchanged.

```java
   * Summary: Assigns a course to a sector, removing it from its current sector

  {
```
    if (courseId == null || courseId.trim().isEmpty() || sectorId == null || sectorId.trim().isEmpty())
    {
      return false;
    }
    Course c = null;
    for (Course existing : getCourses())
    {
      if (courseId.equals(existing.getId()))
      {
        c = existing;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (c == null)

    Sector s = null;

        break;

    if (s == null)
    {
      return false;

    if (c.getSector() != null && c.getSector() != s)
    {
      c.getSector().removeCourse(c);
    }
    s.addCourse(c);
    return true;
  }

  public EList<Session> addSession(final LocalDate date)
  {
    if (date == null)
    {
      return getSessions();
    }
    for (Session s : getSessions())
    {
      if (date.equals(s.getDate()))
      {
        return getSessions();
      }
    }
    Session newSession = TrainingSchoolFactory.eINSTANCE.createSession();
    newSession.setDate(date);
    getSessions().add(newSession);
    return getSessions();
  }

   * Summary: Cancels (removes) the session of this course that occurs on the

   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If date is null, return false.
   *   2. Find the session s in sessions whose date equals the given date.
   *   3. If no such session is found, return false.
   *   4. If s.registeredParticipants is not empty, return false

   * @model dateDataType="edu.trainingSchool.LocalDate" dateRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public boolean cancelSession(final LocalDate date)
  {
    if (date == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    for (Session s : getSessions())
    {
      if (date.equals(s.getDate()))
      {
        if (!s.getRegisteredParticipants().isEmpty())
        {
          return false;
        }
        getSessions().remove(s);
        return true;
      }
    }
    return false;
  }

   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"

      return false;
    }
    for (Document d : getDocuments())
    {
      if (d.getName() != null && d.getName().equals(doc.getName()))
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    getDocuments().add(doc);
    return true;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
